```
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: """A small span tracer that writes JSONL and optionally feeds OpenTelemetry.
2: 
3: The tracer is intentionally dependency-light: JSONL files are always produced,
4: while OTel spans ride along through the API that ``mcp`` already depends on.
5: Installing the SDK/exporter (``--otel console|otlp``) is what makes them visible.
6: """
7: 
8: from __future__ import annotations
9: 
10: import json
11: import time
12: import uuid
13: from collections.abc import Iterator
14: from contextlib import contextmanager
15: from contextvars import ContextVar
16: from dataclasses import dataclass, field
17: from datetime import UTC, datetime
18: from pathlib import Path
19: from typing import Any
20: 
21: from .logging import get_logger
22: 
23: try:  # opentelemetry-api ships with mcp, but never let tracing break the app
24:     from opentelemetry import context as otel_context
25:     from opentelemetry import trace as otel_trace
26:     from opentelemetry.trace import Status, StatusCode
27: 
28:     _OTEL_AVAILABLE = True
29: except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - defensive
30:     _OTEL_AVAILABLE = False
31: 
32: log = get_logger("tracer")
33: 
34: _CURRENT_SPAN: ContextVar[Span | None] = ContextVar("react_mcp_current_span", default=None)
35: _CURRENT_OTEL: ContextVar[Any | None] = ContextVar("react_mcp_current_otel", default=None)
36: 
37: 
38: def _utc_now() -> str:
39:     return datetime.now(UTC).isoformat()
40: 
41: 
42: def _jsonable(value: Any) -> Any:
43:     if isinstance(value, (str, int, float, bool)) or value is None:
44:         return value
45:     if isinstance(value, Path):
46:         return str(value)
47:     if isinstance(value, dict):
48:         return {str(k): _jsonable(v) for k, v in value.items()}
49:     if isinstance(value, (list, tuple, set)):
50:         return [_jsonable(v) for v in value]
51:     return str(value)
52: 
53: 
54: def _otel_attributes(attributes: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
55:     cleaned: dict[str, Any] = {}
56:     for key, value in attributes.items():
57:         if isinstance(value, (str, bool, int, float)):
58:             cleaned[key] = value
59:         elif isinstance(value, (list, tuple)) and all(
60:             isinstance(item, (str, bool, int, float)) for item in value
61:         ):
62:             cleaned[key] = list(value)
63:         else:
64:             cleaned[key] = str(value)
65:     return cleaned
66: 
67: 
68: @dataclass
69: class Span:
70:     """One timed unit of work."""
71: 
72:     trace_id: str
73:     span_id: str
74:     parent_id: str | None
75:     name: str
76:     kind: str
77:     start_ns: int
78:     attributes: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
79:     events: list[dict[str, Any]] = field(default_factory=list)
80:     start_time: str = field(default_factory=_utc_now)
81:     end_time: str | None = None
82:     end_ns: int | None = None
83:     status: str = "unset"
84:     error: str | None = None
85: 
86:     @property
87:     def duration_ms(self) -> float:
88:         if self.end_ns is None:
89:             return (time.perf_counter_ns() - self.start_ns) / 1_000_000
90:         return (self.end_ns - self.start_ns) / 1_000_000
91: 
92:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
93:         return {
94:             "trace_id": self.trace_id,
95:             "span_id": self.span_id,
96:             "parent_id": self.parent_id,
97:             "name": self.name,
98:             "kind": self.kind,
99:             "start_time": self.start_time,
100:             "end_time": self.end_time,
101:             "duration_ms": round(self.duration_ms, 3),
102:             "status": self.status,
103:             "error": self.error,
104:             "attributes": _jsonable(self.attributes),
105:             "events": _jsonable(self.events),
106:         }
107: 
108: 
109: class Tracer:
110:     """Creates spans, writes ``spans.jsonl``/``events.jsonl``/``protocol.jsonl``."""
111: 
112:     def __init__(
113:         self,
114:         trace_dir: Path,
115:         *,
116:         run_name: str = "run",
117:         metadata: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
118:         echo_protocol: bool = False,
119:     ) -> None:
120:         self.trace_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
121:         stamp = datetime.now(UTC).strftime("%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ")
122:         self.run_id = f"{stamp}-{self.trace_id[:8]}"
123:         self.run_dir = trace_dir / self.run_id
124:         self.run_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
125: 
126:         self.spans_path = self.run_dir / "spans.jsonl"
127:         self.events_path = self.run_dir / "events.jsonl"
128:         self.protocol_path = self.run_dir / "protocol.jsonl"
129:         self.run_path = self.run_dir / "run.json"
130:         self.summary_path = self.run_dir / "summary.json"
131: 
132:         self.run_name = run_name
133:         self.echo_protocol = echo_protocol
134:         self.spans: list[Span] = []
135:         self._closed = False
136:         self._otel_tracer = otel_trace.get_tracer("react_mcp") if _OTEL_AVAILABLE else None
137: 
138:         self._write_json(
139:             self.run_path,
140:             {
141:                 "run_id": self.run_id,
142:                 "trace_id": self.trace_id,
143:                 "run_name": run_name,
144:                 "started_at": _utc_now(),
145:                 "metadata": _jsonable(metadata or {}),
146:             },
147:         )
148:         log.debug("trace started run_id=%s dir=%s", self.run_id, self.run_dir)
149: 
150:     # -- spans ------------------------------------------------------------
151:     @contextmanager
152:     def span(self, name: str, *, kind: str = "internal", **attributes: Any) -> Iterator[Span]:
153:         parent = _CURRENT_SPAN.get()
154:         span = Span(
155:             trace_id=self.trace_id,
156:             span_id=uuid.uuid4().hex[:16],
157:             parent_id=parent.span_id if parent else None,
158:             name=name,
159:             kind=kind,
160:             start_ns=time.perf_counter_ns(),
161:             attributes=dict(attributes),
162:         )
163: 
164:         otel_span = None
165:         otel_token = None
166:         if self._otel_tracer is not None:
167:             otel_span = self._otel_tracer.start_span(
168:                 name,
169:                 kind=self._otel_kind(kind),
170:                 attributes=_otel_attributes(span.attributes),
171:             )
172:             otel_token = otel_context.attach(otel_trace.set_span_in_context(otel_span))
173: 
174:         span_token = _CURRENT_SPAN.set(span)
175:         otel_ctx_token = _CURRENT_OTEL.set(otel_span)
176:         try:
177:             yield span
178:         except Exception as exc:
179:             span.status = "error"
180:             span.error = f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
181:             if otel_span is not None:
182:                 otel_span.record_exception(exc)
183:                 otel_span.set_status(Status(StatusCode.ERROR, str(exc)))
184:             raise
185:         else:
186:             if span.status == "unset":
187:                 span.status = "ok"
188:         finally:
189:             span.end_ns = time.perf_counter_ns()
190:             span.end_time = _utc_now()
191:             self.spans.append(span)
192:             self._append_jsonl(self.spans_path, span.to_dict())
193:             if otel_span is not None:
194:                 otel_span.set_attribute("duration_ms", round(span.duration_ms, 3))
195:                 if span.status == "ok":
196:                     otel_span.set_status(Status(StatusCode.OK))
197:                 otel_span.end()
198:             _CURRENT_OTEL.reset(otel_ctx_token)
199:             _CURRENT_SPAN.reset(span_token)
200:             if otel_token is not None:
201:                 otel_context.detach(otel_token)
202: 
203:     @staticmethod
204:     def _otel_kind(kind: str) -> Any:
205:         if not _OTEL_AVAILABLE:
206:             return None
207:         mapping = {
208:             "internal": otel_trace.SpanKind.INTERNAL,
209:             "client": otel_trace.SpanKind.CLIENT,
210:             "server": otel_trace.SpanKind.SERVER,
211:         }
212:         return mapping.get(kind, otel_trace.SpanKind.INTERNAL)
213: 
214:     def event(self, name: str, **attributes: Any) -> None:
215:         """Attach a timestamped event to the current span and the event log."""
216:         current = _CURRENT_SPAN.get()
217:         record = {
218:             "trace_id": self.trace_id,
219:             "span_id": current.span_id if current else None,
220:             "timestamp": _utc_now(),
221:             "name": name,
222:             "attributes": _jsonable(attributes),
223:         }
224:         if current is not None:
225:             current.events.append(record)
226:         self._append_jsonl(self.events_path, record)
227: 
228:         otel_span = _CURRENT_OTEL.get()
229:         if otel_span is not None:
230:             otel_span.add_event(name, attributes=_otel_attributes(attributes))
231: 
232:     # -- protocol frames --------------------------------------------------
233:     def protocol(self, direction: str, message: dict[str, Any], *, echo: bool | None = None) -> None:
234:         """Record one raw JSON-RPC frame (used by the educational raw client)."""
235:         record = {
236:             "timestamp": _utc_now(),
237:             "direction": direction,
238:             "message": _jsonable(message),
239:         }
240:         self._append_jsonl(self.protocol_path, record)
241: 
242:         should_echo = self.echo_protocol if echo is None else echo
243:         if should_echo:
244:             try:
245:                 from .render import print_protocol_frame
246: 
247:                 print_protocol_frame(direction, message)
248:             except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - rendering is best effort
249:                 log.debug("protocol %s %s", direction, message)
250: 
251:     # -- lifecycle --------------------------------------------------------
252:     def summary(self, metrics: dict[str, Any] | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
253:         by_name: dict[str, int] = {}
254:         errors = 0
255:         for span in self.spans:
256:             by_name[span.name] = by_name.get(span.name, 0) + 1
257:             if span.status == "error":
258:                 errors += 1
259:         return {
260:             "run_id": self.run_id,
261:             "trace_id": self.trace_id,
262:             "run_name": self.run_name,
263:             "spans": len(self.spans),
264:             "span_errors": errors,
265:             "spans_by_name": by_name,
266:             "metrics": metrics or {},
267:             "artifacts": {
268:                 "run": str(self.run_path),
269:                 "spans": str(self.spans_path),
270:                 "events": str(self.events_path),
271:                 "protocol": str(self.protocol_path),
272:             },
273:         }
274: 
275:     def close(self, metrics: dict[str, Any] | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
276:         summary = self.summary(metrics)
277:         if not self._closed:
278:             summary["finished_at"] = _utc_now()
279:             self._write_json(self.summary_path, summary)
280:             self._closed = True
281:         return summary
282: 
283:     # -- helpers ----------------------------------------------------------
284:     @staticmethod
285:     def _append_jsonl(path: Path, payload: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
286:         with path.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
287:             handle.write(json.dumps(payload, ensure_ascii=False, default=str) + "\n")
288: 
289:     @staticmethod
290:     def _write_json(path: Path, payload: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
291:         path.write_text(
292:             json.dumps(payload, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False, default=str) + "\n",
293:             encoding="utf-8",
294:         )

(End of file - total 294 lines)
```

<type>file</type>
<content>
1: """Parser for text-based ReAct output.
2: 
3: The model is asked for one of two formats. Real models occasionally wrap JSON in
4: markdown fences, add prose around it, or emit Python-style single quotes, so the
5: parser is deliberately forgiving before the agent decides what to do.
6: """
7: 
8: from __future__ import annotations
9: 
10: import ast
11: import json
12: import re
13: from dataclasses import dataclass
14: from typing import Any, Literal
15: 
16: _FINAL_RE = re.compile(r"final\s*answer\s*:\s*", re.IGNORECASE)
17: _ACTION_RE = re.compile(
18:     r"^\s*action\s*:\s*([A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.\-/]*)\s*$",
19:     re.IGNORECASE | re.MULTILINE,
20: )
21: _ACTION_INLINE_RE = re.compile(
22:     r"\baction\s*:\s*([A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.\-/]*)",
23:     re.IGNORECASE,
24: )
25: _INPUT_RE = re.compile(r"action\s*input\s*:\s*", re.IGNORECASE)
26: _THOUGHT_RE = re.compile(r"thought\s*:\s*", re.IGNORECASE)
27: _OBSERVATION_RE = re.compile(r"\n\s*observation\s*:", re.IGNORECASE)
28: 
29: 
30: @dataclass(slots=True)
31: class ParseResult:
32:     kind: Literal["final", "action", "invalid"]
33:     thought: str | None = None
34:     action: str | None = None
35:     arguments: Any = None
36:     raw_input: str | None = None
37:     final_answer: str | None = None
38:     raw_output: str = ""
39:     error: str | None = None
40: 
41: 
42: def _strip_outer_fence(text: str) -> str:
43:     stripped = text.strip()
44:     if not stripped.startswith("```"):
45:         return stripped
46:     lines = stripped.splitlines()
47:     if lines and lines[0].lstrip().startswith("```"):
48:         lines = lines[1:]
49:     if lines and lines[-1].strip().startswith("```"):
50:         lines = lines[:-1]
51:     return "\n".join(lines).strip()
52: 
53: 
54: def _thought_before(text: str, index: int) -> str | None:
55:     matches = list(_THOUGHT_RE.finditer(text, 0, index))
56:     if matches:
57:         return text[matches[-1].end() : index].strip() or None
58:     prefix = text[:index].strip()
59:     return prefix or None
60: 
61: 
62: def _extract_first_json(text: str) -> str | None:
63:     """Return the first balanced JSON object/array, ignoring braces in strings."""
64:     start = next((i for i, char in enumerate(text) if char in "{["), None)
65:     if start is None:
66:         return None
67:     stack: list[str] = []
68:     in_string = False
69:     escaped = False
70:     pairs = {"}": "{", "]": "["}
71:     for index in range(start, len(text)):
72:         char = text[index]
73:         if in_string:
74:             if escaped:
75:                 escaped = False
76:             elif char == "\\":
77:                 escaped = True
78:             elif char == '"':
79:                 in_string = False
80:             continue
81:         if char == '"':
82:             in_string = True
83:         elif char in "{[":
84:             stack.append(char)
85:         elif char in "}]":
86:             if not stack or stack[-1] != pairs[char]:
87:                 return None
88:             stack.pop()
89:             if not stack:
90:                 return text[start : index + 1]
91:     return None
92: 
93: 
94: def _maybe_unwrap_json(value: Any) -> Any:
95:     if isinstance(value, str) and value.strip()[:1] in {"{", "["}:
96:         try:
97:             return json.loads(value)
98:         except json.JSONDecodeError:
99:             return value
100:     return value
101: 
102: 
103: def parse_action_input(raw: str) -> tuple[Any, str | None]:
104:     """Parse the action input text. Returns ``(value, error)``.
105: 
106:     On failure ``value`` is ``None`` and the caller may still use the raw text
107:     when the tool takes exactly one string argument.
108:     """
109:     if not raw.strip():
110:         return {}, None
111: 
112:     candidates = [raw.strip()]
113:     extracted = _extract_first_json(raw)
114:     if extracted and extracted != candidates[0]:
115:         candidates.append(extracted)
116: 
117:     last_error: str | None = None
118:     for candidate in candidates:
119:         try:
120:             return _maybe_unwrap_json(json.loads(candidate)), None
121:         except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
122:             last_error = f"invalid JSON ({exc.msg} at position {exc.pos})"
123:         try:
124:             value = ast.literal_eval(candidate)
125:         except (ValueError, SyntaxError):
126:             continue
127:         if isinstance(value, (dict, list, int, float, bool)) or value is None:
128:             return _maybe_unwrap_json(value), None
129: 
130:     return None, last_error or "Action Input is not valid JSON."
131: 
132: 
133: def parse_react_output(text: str) -> ParseResult:
134:     """Parse one model turn into a final answer, a tool action, or an error."""
135:     raw = text or ""
136:     cleaned = _strip_outer_fence(raw)
137:     final_match = _FINAL_RE.search(cleaned)
138:     action_match = _ACTION_RE.search(cleaned)
139:     if action_match is None:
140:         inline = _ACTION_INLINE_RE.search(cleaned)
141:         if inline and inline.group(1).lower() != "input":
142:             action_match = inline
143: 
144:     if final_match and (action_match is None or final_match.start() < action_match.start()):
145:         answer = cleaned[final_match.end() :].strip()
146:         answer = _strip_outer_fence(answer)
147:         if answer:
148:             return ParseResult(
149:                 kind="final",
150:                 thought=_thought_before(cleaned, final_match.start()),
151:                 final_answer=answer,
152:                 raw_output=raw,
153:             )
154: 
155:     if action_match is not None:
156:         action = action_match.group(1).strip()
157:         input_match = _INPUT_RE.search(cleaned, action_match.end())
158:         raw_input = ""
159:         if input_match is not None:
160:             raw_input = cleaned[input_match.end() :]
161:         raw_input = _OBSERVATION_RE.split(raw_input, maxsplit=1)[0]
162:         raw_input = _strip_outer_fence(raw_input).strip()
163:         arguments, error = parse_action_input(raw_input)
164:         return ParseResult(
165:             kind="action",
166:             thought=_thought_before(cleaned, action_match.start()),
167:             action=action,
168:             arguments=arguments,
169:             raw_input=raw_input,
170:             raw_output=raw,
171:             error=error,
172:         )
173: 
174:     thought = None
175:     thought_match = _THOUGHT_RE.search(cleaned)
176:     if thought_match:
177:         thought = cleaned[thought_match.end() :].strip() or None
178:     return ParseResult(
179:         kind="invalid",
180:         thought=thought,
181:         raw_output=raw,
182:         error="No 'Action:' or 'Final Answer:' was found.",
183:     )

```
1: """Ollama chat wrapper with tracing and token accounting."""
2: 
3: from __future__ import annotations
4: 
5: import time
6: from dataclasses import dataclass
7: from typing import Any
8: 
9: import httpx
10: from ollama import AsyncClient, ResponseError
11: 
12: from ..config import Settings
13: from ..observability.logging import get_logger
14: from ..observability.tracer import Tracer
15: 
16: log = get_logger("llm")
17: 
18: 
19: class LLMError(RuntimeError):
20:     """A model call failed in a way the user can fix."""
21: 
22: 
23: @dataclass(slots=True)
24: class LLMResponse:
25:     content: str
26:     thinking: str | None
27:     prompt_tokens: int
28:     completion_tokens: int
29:     duration_ms: float
30:     done_reason: str | None = None
31: 
32: 
33: class OllamaLLM:
34:     """Async Ollama client for one configured model."""
35: 
36:     def __init__(self, settings: Settings, *, tracer: Tracer | None = None) -> None:
37:         self.settings = settings
38:         self.tracer = tracer
39:         self.client = AsyncClient(host=settings.ollama_host, timeout=settings.request_timeout)
40: 
41:     async def list_models(self) -> list[str]:
42:         try:
43:             response = await self.client.list()
44:         except Exception as exc:  # pragma: no cover - environmental
45:             raise LLMError(self._explain(exc)) from exc
46:         models = getattr(response, "models", None) or []
47:         names: list[str] = []
48:         for model in models:
49:             name = getattr(model, "model", None) or getattr(model, "name", None)
50:             if name:
51:                 names.append(str(name))
52:         return names
53: 
54:     async def chat(
55:         self,
56:         messages: list[dict[str, str]],
57:         *,
58:         stop: list[str] | None = None,
59:         temperature: float | None = None,
60:     ) -> LLMResponse:
61:         options: dict[str, Any] = {
62:             "temperature": self.settings.temperature if temperature is None else temperature,
63:             "num_ctx": self.settings.num_ctx,
64:         }
65:         if stop:
66:             options["stop"] = stop
67: 
68:         attributes = {
69:             "gen_ai.system": "ollama",
70:             "gen_ai.request.model": self.settings.model,
71:             "message_count": len(messages),
72:             "prompt_chars": sum(len(message.get("content", "")) for message in messages),
73:             "temperature": options["temperature"],
74:             "num_ctx": options["num_ctx"],
75:             "think": self.settings.think,
76:         }
77: 
78:         def _call() -> Any:
79:             return self._chat_call(messages, options)
80: 
81:         if self.tracer is not None:
82:             with self.tracer.span("llm.chat", kind="client", **attributes) as span:
83:                 response, duration_ms = await self._run(_call())
84:                 result = self._to_response(response, duration_ms)
85:                 span.attributes.update(
86:                     {
87:                         "gen_ai.usage.input_tokens": result.prompt_tokens,
88:                         "gen_ai.usage.output_tokens": result.completion_tokens,
89:                         "duration_ms": round(result.duration_ms, 2),
90:                         "response_chars": len(result.content),
91:                     }
92:                 )
93:                 self.tracer.event(
94:                     "llm.response",
95:                     model=self.settings.model,
96:                     prompt_tokens=result.prompt_tokens,
97:                     completion_tokens=result.completion_tokens,
98:                     done_reason=result.done_reason,
99:                 )
100:         else:
101:             response, duration_ms = await self._run(_call())
102:             result = self._to_response(response, duration_ms)
103: 
104:         log.debug(
105:             "llm response model=%s prompt_tokens=%s completion_tokens=%s duration_ms=%.0f",
106:             self.settings.model,
107:             result.prompt_tokens,
108:             result.completion_tokens,
109:             result.duration_ms,
110:         )
111:         return result
112: 
113:     def _chat_call(self, messages: list[dict[str, str]], options: dict[str, Any]) -> Any:
114:         return self.client.chat(
115:             model=self.settings.model,
116:             messages=messages,
117:             stream=False,
118:             think=self.settings.think,
119:             options=options,
120:             keep_alive=self.settings.keep_alive,
121:         )
122: 
123:     async def _run(self, awaitable: Any) -> tuple[Any, float]:
124:         started = time.perf_counter()
125:         try:
126:             response = await awaitable
127:         except Exception as exc:
128:             raise LLMError(self._explain(exc)) from exc
129:         return response, (time.perf_counter() - started) * 1000
130: 
131:     def _to_response(self, response: Any, duration_ms: float) -> LLMResponse:
132:         message = getattr(response, "message", None)
133:         content = (getattr(message, "content", None) or "").strip()
134:         thinking = getattr(message, "thinking", None)
135:         if not content and thinking:
136:             log.warning("model returned empty content; falling back to its thinking text")
137:             content = str(thinking).strip()
138:         return LLMResponse(
139:             content=content,
140:             thinking=thinking,
141:             prompt_tokens=int(getattr(response, "prompt_eval_count", 0) or 0),
142:             completion_tokens=int(getattr(response, "eval_count", 0) or 0),
143:             duration_ms=duration_ms,
144:             done_reason=getattr(response, "done_reason", None),
145:         )
146: 
147:     def _explain(self, exc: Exception) -> str:
148:         host = self.settings.ollama_host
149:         model = self.settings.model
150:         if isinstance(exc, ResponseError):
151:             text = str(exc)
152:             if "not found" in text.lower():
153:                 return (
154:                     f"Ollama could not find model {model!r}. Run `ollama list` and "
155:                     f"`ollama pull {model}`, or pass --model with an installed model."
156:                 )
157:             return f"Ollama returned an error: {text}"
158:         if isinstance(exc, (httpx.ConnectError, httpx.ConnectTimeout)):
159:             return (
160:                 f"Could not connect to Ollama at {host}. Start it with `ollama serve` "
161:                 "or set REACT_MCP_OLLAMA_HOST."
162:             )
163:         if isinstance(exc, httpx.ReadTimeout):
164:             return (
165:                 f"Ollama timed out after {self.settings.request_timeout}s. "
166:                 "Try a smaller model, a smaller --num-ctx, or a larger timeout."
167:             )
168:         return f"Ollama call failed: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
```

```
1: """Shared client types.
2: 
3: The agent only depends on this small interface, so the official SDK client and
4: the hand-rolled protocol client are interchangeable.
5: """
6: 
7: from __future__ import annotations
8: 
9: import json
10: from dataclasses import dataclass, field
11: from typing import Any, Protocol, runtime_checkable
12: 
13: 
14: class MCPClientError(RuntimeError):
15:     """Raised for client-side transport or protocol failures."""
16: 
17: 
18: @dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
19: class ToolSpec:
20:     """Normalized view of an MCP tool definition."""
21: 
22:     name: str
23:     title: str | None
24:     description: str
25:     input_schema: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
26: 
27:     @property
28:     def display_name(self) -> str:
29:         return self.title or self.name
30: 
31:     @property
32:     def required(self) -> list[str]:
33:         return list(self.input_schema.get("required", []))
34: 
35:     @property
36:     def properties(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
37:         return dict(self.input_schema.get("properties", {}))
38: 
39: 
40: @dataclass(slots=True)
41: class ToolResult:
42:     """Normalized result of an MCP ``tools/call``."""
43: 
44:     content: str
45:     is_error: bool = False
46:     structured: Any = None
47: 
48:     @property
49:     def ok(self) -> bool:
50:         return not self.is_error
51: 
52:     def as_observation(self, limit: int | None = None) -> str:
53:         text = self.content
54:         if not text and self.structured is not None:
55:             text = json.dumps(self.structured, ensure_ascii=False, default=str)
56:         text = text.strip() or "(empty result)"
57:         if limit is not None and len(text) > limit:
58:             text = text[:limit] + f"\n[truncated at {limit} characters]"
59:         return text
60: 
61: 
62: @runtime_checkable
63: class MCPToolClient(Protocol):
64:     """What the agent needs from an MCP client."""
65: 
66:     async def list_tools(self) -> list[ToolSpec]: ...
67: 
68:     async def call_tool(self, name: str, arguments: dict[str, Any]) -> ToolResult: ...
69: 
70: 
71: def content_blocks_to_text(blocks: list[Any]) -> str:
72:     """Join MCP content blocks into the text a model should read.
73: 
74:     Accepts both SDK objects (``TextContent``) and raw JSON dictionaries, so the
75:     same helper works for the official client and the hand-rolled one.
76:     """
77:     parts: list[str] = []
78:     for block in blocks or []:
79:         if isinstance(block, dict):
80:             text = block.get("text")
81:             if isinstance(text, str):
82:                 parts.append(text)
83:                 continue
84:             if "data" in block:
85:                 mime = block.get("mimeType") or block.get("mime_type") or "binary"
86:                 parts.append(f"[{mime} content, {len(str(block['data']))} chars]")
87:                 continue
88:             parts.append(json.dumps(block, ensure_ascii=False, default=str))
89:             continue
90: 
91:         text = getattr(block, "text", None)
92:         if isinstance(text, str):
93:             parts.append(text)
94:             continue
95:         data = getattr(block, "data", None)
96:         if data is not None:
97:             mime = getattr(block, "mime_type", None) or getattr(block, "mimeType", "binary")
98:             parts.append(f"[{mime} content, {len(str(data))} chars]")
99:             continue
100:         parts.append(str(block))
101:     return "\n".join(parts).strip()
```

```
1: """Per-run counters and timings."""
2: 
3: from __future__ import annotations
4: 
5: import time
6: from dataclasses import dataclass, field
7: 
8: 
9: @dataclass
10: class RunMetrics:
11:     """Mutable counters collected while a run is in progress."""
12: 
13:     llm_calls: int = 0
14:     prompt_tokens: int = 0
15:     completion_tokens: int = 0
16:     tool_calls: int = 0
17:     tool_errors: int = 0

19:     steps: int = 0

21:     tool_duration_ms: float = 0.0
22:     started_perf: float = field(default_factory=time.perf_counter, repr=False)
23:     wall_time_ms: float = 0.0
24: 
25:     def record_llm(
26:         self, *, prompt_tokens: int | None, completion_tokens: int | None, duration_ms: float
27:     ) -> None:
28:         self.llm_calls += 1
29:         self.prompt_tokens += prompt_tokens or 0
30:         self.completion_tokens += completion_tokens or 0
31:         self.llm_duration_ms += duration_ms
32: 
33:     def record_tool(self, *, duration_ms: float, is_error: bool) -> None:
34:         self.tool_calls += 1
35:         self.tool_duration_ms += duration_ms
36:         if is_error:
37:             self.tool_errors += 1
38: 
39:     def finish(self) -> None:
40:         self.wall_time_ms = (time.perf_counter() - self.started_perf) * 1000.0
41: 
42:     @property
43:     def total_tokens(self) -> int:
44:         return self.prompt_tokens + self.completion_tokens
45:
```

## Assistant (Build · Big Pickle · 5.2s)

_Thinking:_

Parser behavior: final answer includes subsequent lines. Is that desirable? A final answer followed by an action should probably stop at action. The model output could have Final Answer then extra. Test expectation reasonable: final answer should be "done" and ignore later action. But maybe model writes multiline final answer; truncating at Action is good. Implement: when final_match before action_match, cut answer at action_match.start() and also at `\nObservation:`? Final answer could legitimately contain "Action:"? Unlikely. Better:
```python
end = action_match.start() if action_match and action_match.start() > final_match.end() else len(cleaned)
answer = cleaned[final_match.end():end].strip()
```
But action_match may be inline and inside final answer text; e.g., final answer "You should take Action: X" would truncate incorrectly. However parser already uses inline action detection which can misfire. For our test, action on own line. Use only line-anchored action? `_ACTION_RE` (line anchored). We have `action_match` maybe inline fallback. For final precedence, we can use `_ACTION_RE.search` only (block action) to truncate. Let's track `block_action = _ACTION_RE.search(cleaned)`, and if final before block action, cut. Simpler:

```python
final_match = _FINAL_RE.search(cleaned)
block_action = _ACTION_RE.search(cleaned)
...
if final_match and (action_match is None or final_match.start() < action_match.start()):
    end = block_action.start() if block_action and block_action.start() > final_match.end() else len(cleaned)
    answer = cleaned[final_match.end():end].strip()
```
But `action_match` may be inline due to `Final Answer: done Action: calculator`; then final start before action, and block_action None; answer includes "done Action: calculator". Rare. Fine.

Alternatively test should expect full text? No, better parser fix.

Apply.

**Tool: edit**

**Input:**
```json
{
  "filePath": "/Users/ajayz/Documents/AZ_Code/05_AI_ML/AI_Agents/ReActAgentWithMCP/src/react_mcp/agent/parser.py",
  "oldString": "    cleaned = _strip_outer_fence(raw)\n    final_match = _FINAL_RE.search(cleaned)\n    action_match = _ACTION_RE.search(cleaned)\n    if action_match is None:\n        inline = _ACTION_INLINE_RE.search(cleaned)\n        if inline and inline.group(1).lower() != \"input\":\n            action_match = inline\n\n    if final_match and (action_match is None or final_match.start() < action_match.start()):\n        answer = cleaned[final_match.end() :].strip()\n        answer = _strip_outer_fence(answer)",
  "newString": "    cleaned = _strip_outer_fence(raw)\n    final_match = _FINAL_RE.search(cleaned)\n    block_action = _ACTION_RE.search(cleaned)\n    action_match = block_action\n    if action_match is None:\n        inline = _ACTION_INLINE_RE.search(cleaned)\n        if inline and inline.group(1).lower() != \"input\":\n            action_match = inline\n\n    if final_match and (action_match is None or final_match.start() < action_match.start()):\n        end = len(cleaned)\n        if block_action is not None and block_action.start() > final_match.end():\n            end = block_action.start()\n        answer = cleaned[final_match.end() : end].strip()\n        answer = _strip_outer_fence(answer)"
}
```

```json
{
```

```json
  "content": "# ReAct + MCP with a local model\n\nA small, runnable system for learning how an agent actually works. It connects\nfour pieces: a local Ollama model, a text-based ReAct loop, an MCP client, and\nan MCP server with deterministic tools. Every model call, tool call, and raw\nJSON-RPC frame can be traced on disk.\n\n```text\n                 +------------------+\n  question ----> |   ReActAgent     | <----> Ollama (qwen3.5:9b)\n                 |  Thought/Action  |\n                 +---------+--------+\n                           |\n                     MCPToolClient\n                     /            \\\n             SDKMCPClient     RawMCPClient\n                     \\            /\n                      \\          /\n                   JSON-RPC over stdio\n                           |\n                 +---------v--------+\n                 |    MCP server    |\n                 | calculator, time |\n                 | notes, files     |\n                 | resources/prompts|\n                 +------------------+\n```\n\nThe agent uses **text ReAct**, not native tool calling, so the reasoning and\nevery action are visible in the transcript and trace. The server is local and\nsandboxed: no network tools, no arbitrary code execution, and file access is\nrestricted to `data/workspace`.\n\n## Requirements\n\n- macOS or Linux with Python 3.12+\n- [uv](https://docs.astral.sh/uv/)\n- [Ollama](https://ollama.com/) running locally, with at least one model\n\n```bash\nollama list\nollama pull qwen3.5:9b\n```\n\n## Quick start\n\n```bash\nuv sync --all-extras\ncp .env.example .env          # optional\nuv run react-mcp doctor\nuv run react-mcp tools\nuv run react-mcp ask \"What is (17 * 3) + 9, and what time is it in UTC?\"\n```\n\n`ask` prints each Thought, Action, and Observation, then a final answer and run\nmetrics. Use `--quiet` for just the answer.\n\n## Commands\n\n| Command | What it does |\n| --- | --- |\n| `react-mcp doctor` | Check Python, Ollama, the model, and the MCP server. |\n| `react-mcp tools` | Connect over MCP and list tools, resources, and prompts. |\n| `react-mcp call <tool> --args '<json>'` | Call one tool directly, without the LLM. |\n| `react-mcp ask \"<question>\"` | Run the ReAct agent once. |\n| `react-mcp repl` | Interactive agent session (`:tools`, `:help`, `:exit`). |\n| `react-mcp protocol-demo` | Print the raw JSON-RPC frames of a full MCP session. |\n| `react-mcp serve` | Run the MCP server over stdio. |\n| `react-mcp serve --http --port 8000` | Run it over Streamable HTTP. |\n\nCommon flags go after the subcommand:\n\n```bash\nuv run react-mcp ask \"...\" --model llama3.2:latest --max-steps 6\nuv run react-mcp ask \"...\" --no-trace --quiet\nuv run react-mcp ask \"...\" --think                  # models with a thinking channel\nuv run react-mcp tools --transport http --server-url http://127.0.0.1:8000/mcp\nuv run react-mcp ask \"...\" --otel console           # requires the otel extra\n```\n\nExamples worth trying:\n\n```bash\nuv run react-mcp ask \"Search the notes for observability and summarize what a span is.\"\nuv run react-mcp ask \"Read the react note and explain why this agent uses text instead of native tool calling.\"\nuv run react-mcp ask \"List the workspace files, then write hello.txt with a short greeting.\"\n```\n\n## How it works\n\n1. `SDKMCPClient` launches `python -m react_mcp.server` over stdio and performs\n   the MCP handshake. It discovers tool names, descriptions, and JSON schemas.\n2. `build_system_prompt` renders those schemas into a ReAct prompt. The model is\n   told to stop at `Observation:` so it cannot invent tool output.\n3. The model replies with a `Thought`, an `Action`, and `Action Input`. The\n   parser is forgiving about code fences, prose, and Python-style quotes.\n4. The agent calls `tools/call` over MCP, appends the observation to the\n   transcript, and loops.\n5. A `Final Answer` ends the run. Step limits, parse retries, and a forced-final\n   pass keep the loop bounded.\n\nThe official SDK client negotiates protocol `2026-07-28` by default. The\neducational raw client in `scripts/demo_protocol.py` performs the legacy\n`initialize` handshake (`2025-11-25`) so the frames match most MCP material.\n\n## MCP surface\n\nTools:\n\n- `calculator` - arithmetic via an AST whitelist, never `eval`.\n- `get_current_time` - IANA timezone, defaults to `UTC`.\n- `search_notes` / `read_note` - search and read `data/notes/*.md`.\n- `list_files` / `read_file` / `write_file` - sandboxed to `data/workspace`.\n\nResources: `notes://index`, `notes://<name>`, `config://agent`.\n\nPrompts: `research(topic)`, `explain_mcp()`.\n\n## Observability\n\nEach run writes a folder under `traces/<timestamp>-<run-id>/`:\n\n| File | Contents |\n| --- | --- |\n| `run.json` | Run id, start time, environment metadata. |\n| `spans.jsonl` | One JSON object per span (nested, with durations). |\n| `events.jsonl` | Standalone timestamped events. |\n| `protocol.jsonl` | Raw MCP frames (protocol demo / raw client). |\n| `summary.json` | Span counts, metrics, artifact paths. |\n\nTypical spans: `agent.run` > `agent.step` > `llm.chat` and `mcp.tools/call`.\nMetrics include steps, LLM calls, prompt/completion tokens, tool calls, tool\nerrors, parse retries, and wall time. `--otel console` or `--otel otlp` also\nexports spans through OpenTelemetry; install with `uv sync --all-extras`.\n\n```bash\nls traces\njq -c '{name, ms:.duration_ms, status}' traces/*/spans.jsonl\n```\n\n## Configuration\n\nSettings come from environment variables with the `REACT_MCP_` prefix, loaded\nfrom `.env` when present. CLI flags override them.\n\n| Variable | Default | Purpose |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `REACT_MCP_MODEL` | `qwen3.5:9b` | Ollama model. |\n| `REACT_MCP_OLLAMA_HOST` | `http://localhost:11434` | Ollama endpoint. |\n| `REACT_MCP_TEMPERATURE` | `0.1` | Sampling temperature. |\n| `REACT_MCP_NUM_CTX` | `8192` | Context window. |\n| `REACT_MCP_THINK` | `false` | Enable thinking-channel output. |\n| `REACT_MCP_MAX_STEPS` | `8` | Maximum ReAct iterations. |\n| `REACT_MCP_MAX_PARSE_RETRIES` | `2` | Format-correction retries. |\n| `REACT_MCP_MAX_OBSERVATION_CHARS` | `2000` | Observation truncation. |\n| `REACT_MCP_TRANSPORT` | `stdio` | `stdio` or `http`. |\n| `REACT_MCP_SERVER_URL` | `http://127.0.0.1:8000/mcp` | HTTP endpoint. |\n| `REACT_MCP_TRACE_DIR` | `./traces` | Trace output directory. |\n| `REACT_MCP_LOG_LEVEL` | `INFO` | Log verbosity. |\n| `REACT_MCP_OTEL` | `none` | `none`, `console`, or `otlp`. |\n| `REACT_MCP_OTLP_ENDPOINT` | `http://localhost:4317` | OTLP collector. |\n\nPath settings (`REACT_MCP_WORKSPACE_DIR`, `REACT_MCP_NOTES_DIR`) are available\ntoo; see `.env.example`.\n\n## Adding a tool\n\nAdd a decorated function in `src/react_mcp/tools.py`, then restart the client so\nit can rediscover the tools:\n\n```python\n@mcp.tool(title=\"Word count\")\n@instrumented\ndef word_count(text: Annotated[str, Field(description=\"Text to count.\")]) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Count whitespace-separated words.\"\"\"\n    return len(text.split())\n```\n\n`@instrumented` is a `functools.wraps` wrapper, so the MCP SDK still sees the\noriginal signature and generates the JSON schema from the annotations.\n\n## Project layout\n\n```text\nsrc/react_mcp/\n  cli.py                 # all CLI subcommands\n  config.py              # pydantic settings and paths\n  server.py              # MCPServer construction and transports\n  tools.py               # tools, resources, prompts, sandbox\n  agent/\n    prompts.py           # ReAct system prompt and retry prompts\n    parser.py            # forgiving Thought/Action/Final parser\n    llm.py               # Ollama wrapper, tokens, friendly errors\n    react.py             # the loop\n  mcp_client/\n    base.py              # shared ToolSpec/ToolResult/Protocol\n    sdk_client.py        # official mcp.Client wrapper\n    raw_client.py        # hand-rolled JSON-RPC stdio client\n  observability/\n    tracer.py            # JSONL spans/events/protocol + OTel bridge\n    metrics.py           # per-run counters\n    render.py            # Rich output\n    logging.py           # stderr logging\n    otel.py              # optional exporter setup\ndata/notes/              # note corpus served over MCP resources/tools\ndata/workspace/          # the only writable directory for file tools\nscripts/demo_protocol.py # standalone raw-frame demo\ntests/                   # unit + subprocess integration tests\ndocs/mcp-protocol.md     # protocol walkthrough\n```\n\n## Testing\n\n```bash\nuv run ruff check .\nuv run pytest -m \"not integration\"   # fast unit tests\nuv run pytest                        # also launches a real MCP subprocess\n```\n\nOllama is not required for the test suite; the agent tests use a scripted fake\nLLM. The `doctor` command and `ask` are the Ollama-dependent checks.\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n- **`Connection refused`** - start Ollama with `ollama serve`.\n- **`model not found`** - run `ollama pull <model>` or pass `--model`.\n- **Malformed action JSON** - lower the temperature, use a larger model, or let\n  the built-in parse retry recover. `--think` can help reasoning at the cost of\n  latency.\n- **Context overflow** - raise `REACT_MCP_NUM_CTX` or lower `--max-steps`.\n- **First call is slow** - the model is loading. Later calls reuse it for\n  `REACT_MCP_KEEP_ALIVE`.\n- **Inspector** - `uv run mcp dev src/react_mcp/server.py` opens the MCP\n  Inspector for the module-level server.\n\n## Safety\n\nThis is a learning project, not a hardened service. Tool arguments are treated\nas untrusted: arithmetic is AST-whitelisted, file paths are resolved and checked\nagainst the workspace root, and writes are size-limited. The HTTP transport has\nno authentication and binds to loopback by default; do not expose it publicly\nwithout adding auth and TLS.\n\nSee [docs/mcp-protocol.md](docs/mcp-protocol.md) for the protocol details and\n[data/notes/](data/notes/) for the notes the agent can search.\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "filePath": "/Users/ajayz/Documents/AZ_Code/05_AI_ML/AI_Agents/ReActAgentWithMCP/docs/mcp-protocol.md",
  "content": "# The MCP protocol, frame by frame\n\nThis is a walkthrough of what this project's client and server actually put on\nthe wire. Run `uv run react-mcp protocol-demo` in another terminal to see the\nsame sequence printed live. Every frame is also appended to\n`traces/<run>/protocol.jsonl`.\n\n## Roles and transports\n\n- **Host** - the application the user interacts with (this CLI).\n- **Client** - one logical connection to one server. Here: `SDKMCPClient` or\n  `RawMCPClient`.\n- **Server** - the process exposing tools, resources, and prompts. Here:\n  `python -m react_mcp.server`.\n\nTwo transports are supported:\n\n| Transport | How it works | When this project uses it |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| stdio | Client launches the server and exchanges newline-delimited JSON on stdin/stdout. stderr is for logs. | Default for `ask`, `tools`, `call`, `repl`, `protocol-demo`. |\n| Streamable HTTP | Client POSTs JSON-RPC messages to an HTTP endpoint; the server may stream responses. | `react-mcp serve --http` plus `--transport http`. |\n\nstdio frames are just UTF-8 JSON followed by `\\n`. There is no length prefix and\nno Content-Length header. A frame must not contain a literal newline, so JSON\nserializers escape newlines inside strings.\n\n## Lifecycle\n\n### 1. initialize\n\nThe client opens with its protocol version, capabilities, and identity:\n\n```json\n{\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"id\":1,\"method\":\"initialize\",\"params\":{\n  \"protocolVersion\":\"2025-11-25\",\n  \"capabilities\":{},\n  \"clientInfo\":{\"name\":\"react-mcp-raw-client\",\"version\":\"0.1.0\"}\n}}\n```\n\nA server that serves the legacy handshake replies:\n\n```json\n{\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"id\":1,\"result\":{\n  \"protocolVersion\":\"2025-11-25\",\n  \"capabilities\":{\n    \"tools\":{\"listChanged\":true},\n    \"resources\":{\"subscribe\":false,\"listChanged\":true},\n    \"prompts\":{\"listChanged\":true},\n    \"logging\":{}\n  },\n  \"serverInfo\":{\"name\":\"ReAct Tools\",\"version\":\"0.1.0\"},\n  \"instructions\":\"Local learning server for a ReAct agent...\"\n}}\n```\n\nThe client must then confirm:\n\n```json\n{\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"method\":\"notifications/initialized\"}\n```\n\nNotifications have **no `id`** and get no response. During initialization the\nclient can also send `notifications/initialized` only after it has received a\nsuccessful `initialize` result.\n\n### 2. Newer discovery\n\nRecent MCP revisions added `server/discover`, which lets a client ask what the\nserver supports before choosing an interaction mode. The official SDK client\nused here negotiates `2026-07-28` automatically; the raw client deliberately\nuses `initialize` with `2025-11-25` because most documentation and tooling still\ndescribe that handshake. The server accepts both eras, which is why the same\nsubprocess works for `SDKMCPClient` and `RawMCPClient`.\n\nThe core rule is unchanged: both sides must agree on a protocol version, and the\nclient must only use capabilities the server advertised.\n\n### 3. Discovery\n\n```json\n{\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"id\":2,\"method\":\"tools/list\",\"params\":{}}\n```\n\n```json\n{\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"id\":2,\"result\":{\"tools\":[\n  {\n    \"name\":\"calculator\",\n    \"title\":\"Calculator\",\n    \"description\":\"Evaluate a basic arithmetic expression safely.\",\n    \"inputSchema\":{\n      \"type\":\"object\",\n      \"properties\":{\"expression\":{\"type\":\"string\",\"description\":\"Arithmetic expression...\"}},\n      \"required\":[\"expression\"]\n    },\n    \"annotations\":{\"readOnlyHint\":true,\"openWorldHint\":false}\n  }\n]}}\n```\n\n`inputSchema` is JSON Schema generated from the Python signature. The agent\nturns it into prompt text, which is why `Annotated[..., Field(description=...)]`\nmatters. `annotations` are hints for hosts, not security boundaries.\n\nList methods may return `nextCursor`; clients should paginate until it is\nabsent. This project has few enough items that it does not need to.\n\nResources and prompts are discovered the same way:\n\n```json\n{\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"id\":3,\"method\":\"resources/list\",\"params\":{}}\n{\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"id\":4,\"method\":\"prompts/list\",\"params\":{}}\n```\n\n### 4. Calling a tool\n\n```json\n{\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"id\":5,\"method\":\"tools/call\",\"params\":{\n  \"name\":\"calculator\",\n  \"arguments\":{\"expression\":\"(17 * 3) + 9\"}\n}}\n```\n\nA successful result returns typed content blocks:\n\n```json\n{\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"id\":5,\"result\":{\n  \"content\":[{\"type\":\"text\",\"text\":\"60\"}],\n  \"structuredContent\":{\"result\":\"60\"},\n  \"isError\":false\n}}\n```\n\n`content` is a list because a tool can return text, images, audio, embedded\nresources, or links. `structuredContent` is optional and is meant for\nmachine-readable output. `isError: true` means the tool ran but reported a\ndomain error (division by zero, bad path); it is still a successful JSON-RPC\nresponse. This project formats `isError` results as\n`Error from tool '...': ...` so the model can recover.\n\n### 5. Reading a resource\n\n```json\n{\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"id\":6,\"method\":\"resources/read\",\"params\":{\"uri\":\"notes://index\"}}\n```\n\n```json\n{\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"id\":6,\"result\":{\"contents\":[\n  {\"uri\":\"notes://index\",\"mimeType\":\"application/json\",\"text\":\"[{\\\"name\\\":\\\"react\\\",...}]\"}\n]}\n```\n\nResources are identified by URI. A server can expose fixed URIs and URI\ntemplates such as `notes://{name}`; the client fills in the variable and reads\nthe resulting URI.\n\n### 6. Rendering a prompt\n\n```json\n{\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"id\":7,\"method\":\"prompts/get\",\"params\":{\n  \"name\":\"explain_mcp\",\"arguments\":{}\n}}\n```\n\n```json\n{\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\",\"id\":7,\"result\":{\"messages\":[\n  {\"role\":\"user\",\"content\":{\"type\":\"text\",\"text\":\"Explain the MCP lifecycle...\"}}\n]}}\n```\n\nPrompts are reusable message templates. The host decides when to insert them\ninto a conversation; the server does not call the model.\n\n### 7. Shutdown\n\nThere is no mandatory goodbye request. The client closes stdin (stdio) or the\nHTTP session, and the server exits. In this project `Client.__aexit__` and\n`RawMCPClient.close()` handle that, including killing a hung subprocess after a\nshort timeout.\n\n## Message shapes\n\nEvery message is a JSON object with `\"jsonrpc\":\"2.0\"`.\n\n| Kind | Has `id`? | Has `method`? | Shape |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Request | yes | yes | `{id, method, params}` |\n| Success response | yes | no | `{id, result}` |\n| Error response | yes | no | `{id, error:{code, message, data?}}` |\n| Notification | no | yes | `{method, params?}` |\n\nStandard error codes include `-32700` (parse error), `-32600` (invalid request),\n`-32601` (method not found), `-32602` (invalid params), and `-32603` (internal\nerror). Server-defined codes must stay outside the JSON-RPC reserved range.\n\n## Capabilities in practice\n\nA server advertises only what it implements. This server advertises:\n\n- `tools` - `tools/list`, `tools/call`\n- `resources` - `resources/list`, `resources/read`, templates\n- `prompts` - `prompts/list`, `prompts/get`\n- `logging` - `notifications/message` when the server emits logs\n\nThe agent depends only on `tools`. `SDKMCPClient.list_resources()` and\n`list_prompts()` are convenience methods for exploration; the ReAct loop never\ncalls them automatically.\n\n## Where this lives in the code\n\n| Concept | Implementation |\n| --- | --- |\n| Server construction and transport | `src/react_mcp/server.py` |\n| Tool/resource/prompt registration | `src/react_mcp/tools.py` |\n| Official-SDK client | `src/react_mcp/mcp_client/sdk_client.py` |\n| Raw JSON-RPC client and framing | `src/react_mcp/mcp_client/raw_client.py` |\n| Local span/protocol trace | `src/react_mcp/observability/tracer.py` |\n| Live frame printer | `src/react_mcp/observability/render.py` |\n\n## Debugging checklist\n\n1. Run `react-mcp doctor` to confirm the model and server.\n2. Run `react-mcp protocol-demo` to see exactly which methods are sent and what\n   comes back.\n3. Look at `protocol.jsonl` for the machine-readable transcript.\n4. Look at `spans.jsonl` for timing and nesting, including `mcp.tools/call`.\n5. If a tool call is rejected, compare `arguments` with the tool's\n   `inputSchema`. The server validates before running your function.\n\n## Security boundaries\n\n- **Validate arguments on the server.** MCP does not provide a trust boundary;\n  a malicious host can send anything.\n- **Never use `eval`.** This project parses arithmetic with `ast` and allows\n  only numeric constants and a fixed operator set.\n- **Confine file access.** `_safe_path` resolves symlinks and rejects absolute\n  paths, `..`, and anything outside `data/workspace`.\n- **Treat resources as data, not instructions.** A note could contain text that\n  tries to redirect the agent; keep the system prompt authoritative.\n- **Bind HTTP to loopback** unless you add authentication and TLS. The HTTP\n  transport here is for local inspection only.\n"
}
```

╰──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
╭──────────────────────────────── Observation ─────────────────────────────────╮
│ # Observability: traces, spans, and metrics                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ An agent is a distributed system in miniature: the LLM call, the MCP client, │
│ the MCP server subprocess, and the tools all participate in one user         │
│ request.                                                                     │
│ Observability means being able to reconstruct that request after the fact.   │
│                                                                              │
│ ## What this project records                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ Each CLI run gets a directory under `traces/<timestamp>-<run-id>/`:          │
│                                                                              │
│ - `run.json` - run id, start time, and environment metadata.                 │
│ - `spans.jsonl` - one JSON object per finished span.                         │
│ - `events.jsonl` - standalone timestamped events, also attached to their     │
│ span.                                                                        │
│ - `protocol.jsonl` - raw MCP JSON-RPC frames, when protocol echo is enabled. │
│ - `summary.json` - span counts, final metrics, and artifact paths.           │
│                                                                              │
│ Spans are nested. A typical `ask` run looks like:                            │
│                                                                              │
│ ```text                                                                      │
│ agent.run                                                                    │
│   agent.step                                                                 │
│     llm.chat                                                                 │
│     mcp.tools/call                                                           │
│   agent.finish                                                               │
│ ```                                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ Each span carries attributes such as the model, tool name, arguments, token  │
│ counts, or parse result. Durations are measured with a monotonic clock.      │
│                                                                              │
│ ## Metrics                                                                   │
│                                                                              │
│ The agent counts steps, LLM calls, prompt/completion tokens, tool calls,     │
│ tool                                                                         │
│ errors, parse retries, and wall-clock time. Token counts come from Ollama;   │
│ they                                                                         │
│ are estimates of model work, not billing units, because the model runs       │
│ locally.                                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ ## Optional OpenTelemetry                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│ The tracer can also emit spans to an OTel exporter. Install the extra with   │
│ `uv sync --all-extras`, then run with `--otel console` or `--otel otlp`. The │
│ local JSONL trace is always the source of truth; OTel is for connecting this │
│ toy to a real backend such as Jaeger, Tempo, or Honeycomb.                   │
│                                                                              │
│ ## Inspecting a run                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ ```bash                                                                      │
│ ls traces                                                                    │
│ jq -c '{name, ms:.duration_ms, status}' traces/*/spans.jsonl                 │
│ ```                                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ Because traces are plain JSONL, they are easy to diff, grep, and feed into a │
│ notebook. Nothing is uploaded anywhere.                                      │
╰──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
╭─────────────────────────────────── Step 3 ───────────────────────────────────╮
│ Thought: I have read the note and found the definition of a span in the      │
│ context of observability. A span represents a single unit of work (like an   │
│ LLM call or tool invocation) within a trace, carrying attributes such as     │
│ duration, status, and metadata.                                              │
│                                                                              │
╰──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
╭─────────────────────────── Final Answer (3 steps) ───────────────────────────╮
│ In the context of observability, a span is a nested unit of work within a    │
│ trace that records details like duration, status, and attributes (e.g.,      │
│ model used, tool called) for a specific operation such as an LLM call or     │
│ tool invocation.                                                             │

```
    protocol-demo       Show raw JSON-RPC MCP frames
```
